- [ ] [type:bug] PDF custom scale not persisted to QSettings [P3] [subject:UX]
  - Details: `_save_settings` reads the hidden raw `_custom_scale_edit` during a PDF-ratio session, so a calibrated/typed custom PDF scale resets to the seed next launch (DXF raw-factor persistence unaffected). Persist `_current_scale()` when `_ratio_fields_active()` + back-solve on restore. `underlay_import_dialog.py`.
- [ ] [type:bug] Duplicate underlay drops import fields [P3] [subject:CAD]
  - Details: `underlay_context_menu._duplicate` copies neither `selected_layers` nor `import_scale`/`import_base_x/y`, so a duplicated PDF renders all layers at a wrong transform. Same class as the Modify layer bug, different entry point. `underlay_context_menu.py`.

  - Details: the "edited name survives `replace_underlay`/`apply_import_params_preserving_management`" path is verified only by reading + unit tests; add an integration test so a future removal of `name` from `_GEOMETRY_PLACEMENT_FIELDS` is caught. `tests/`.
- [ ] [type:feature] Cancel inert during GUI-thread preview-build [P3] [subject:CAD]
  - Details: same root as the spinner stutter, user-accepted. Extraction is cancellable now, but the post-extraction preview-path build blocks the GUI thread so Cancel (and the spinner) freeze there. Real fix = incremental/off-thread preview build (same territory as `underlay-workflow §18` perf work). `underlay_import_dialog.py`.
- [ ] [type:maint] Calibration snap tolerance is strict (2%) [P3] [subject:UX]
  - Details: a metric `1:10.3` measurement won't snap (only ≤~1:10.15). Bump `_SCALE_SNAP_TOL` to 0.03 if real-world picks land further off. `underlay_import_dialog.py`.
- [ ] [type:bug] PDF reload inline-cache key mismatch [P3] [subject:CAD]
  - Details: with a layer subset active, `_import_pdf_vectors` writes the inline cache under a `selected_layers=None` key while the reader keys include `selected_layers`, so the next reload cache-misses and re-extracts once (correct rendering throughout; masked after the first project Save). Fix the write key + tighten the "layer-agnostic cache key" comment. `model_space.py`.
- [ ] [type:maint] Task-1 minor: redundant page-0 PDF worker churn + semi-synthetic error-path test [P3] [subject:Code Quality]
  - Details: redundant page-0 PDF worker churn on a Modify of a non-zero page (two workers superseded before the target); the PDF error-path test is semi-synthetic (calls `_on_pdf_extract_error` directly rather than a raising worker). `underlay_import_dialog.py`.

## PDF/DXF import geometry inflation

- [ ] [type:maint] DXF geometry inflation — bench-first flatten tuning [P2] [subject:CAD]
  - Details: filed 2026-08-30 from task 73; PDF sibling shipped. Bench whether DXF `SPLINE entity.flattening(0.5)` (`dxf_import_worker.py:549`) and the fixed-count ARC/ELLIPSE tessellation (`steps=64`) actually inflate a reference DXF underlay before changing anything. Note the unit gap: DXF tolerance is in drawing units (mm/inch/feet, file-dependent), not paper points — decide unit-normalization (e.g. via `$INSUNITS`/extents) so a fixed number means a fixed plotted deviation. Consider a Preferences knob mirroring the PDF one. Needs a representative DXF underlay to visually gate. `dxf_import_worker.py`, `underlay_cache.py`, `settings/panes.py`. ref: underlay-workflow §18.5.

- [ ] [type:bug] MainWindow close with a plan item selected crashes the test process (exit 127) [P2] [subject:Testing]
  - Details: found 2026-09-30 (closable-3D-tab Task 10), proven pre-existing at `7233649` in a worktree (1/3 plain runs exit 127; hooked run shows the RuntimeError). At `MainWindow.close()` the scene's destruction emits `selectionChanged` → `_on_selection_changed_contextual` → `self.scene.selectedItems()` → "wrapped C/C++ object of type Model_Space has been deleted" inside a Qt slot. Workaround in `tests/test_view3d_tab_mainwindow.py` fixture (clear selection before close). Fix in production: disconnect/guard the contextual slot on teardown (`sip.isdeleted`). Likely one cause of the "native GL degradation" crashes; related to the module-singleton fixture item in this file. `main.py`. ref: test-harness.

## ALIGN placement & misc CAD

- [ ] [type:bug] Doors at ground level (elevation 0) don't render in plan/sheets until a level switch [P2] [subject:CAD]
  - Details: user, 2026-08-27 paper-space-bundle smoke test — NOT yet root-caused; a live-only render bug, needs the running app. Symptom: a door hosted on a ground-level (elevation 0) wall shows a solid wall, no door in plan view AND on sheets; the door is visible in 3D; switching the active level away and back (or nudging the level elevation off 0) makes it appear and it persists. Ruled out (headless, proven): the data/z/visibility/geometry are all correct through the FULL save→`load_from_file`→`apply_to_scene` path — after load the opening is in `wall.openings`, `isVisible()==True`, z-pinned above its host wall (`op.z=30.83 > wall.z=30.78`, the §585 `Z_CAT_OPENING` pin), path non-empty, `z_range=(0,2032)`. `_apply_z_filter` at `[view_depth=-1000, view_height=2000]` keeps it visible. The plan gap is drawn by `WallOpening._paint_symbol` filling `_gap_rect` with the scene-bg colour (needs `op.z>wall.z`, which holds) — NOT `_is_section_cut`-dependent. A MainWindow `_load_project` test asserts the door visible after load and passes (doesn't reproduce). A speculative "build ViewResolver before `_activate_plan_view`" fix was tried and reverted — it's a functional no-op. Leading hypothesis: a live-only stale-paint / missing viewport invalidate on first load. Next (needs the live app): (a) does zoom in/out alone (repaint, no `apply_to_scene`) fix it? → pure stale-paint → force a repaint/`scene.update()` after load; (b) is the door selectable where it should be vs nothing there? Also compare vs `main` to confirm pre-existing vs a bundle regression. `wall_opening.py` (`_paint_symbol`/`_gap_rect`), `model_view.py`, `main.py`, `level_manager.py`.
- [ ] [type:feature] ALIGN: Parallel-OFFSET tracking (perpendicular offset from a reference line) [P2] [subject:CAD]
  - Details: user, 2026-08-26 ALIGN smoke test — the parallel behavior actually wanted. The shipped direction-acquire "Parallel" only constrains direction (draw parallel, length along it) and is defaulted OFF (`ALIGN_DIR_PARALLEL_DEFAULT=False`, flaky track-swap flicker). What the user wants is in-placement OFFSET: acquire a reference line, then a typed distance places your point/line parallel to the reference at that perpendicular offset (AutoCAD OFFSET as a live snapping aid). Distinct feature — needs its own grill→design→build: side selection, snap-to-offset-guide vs type-distance, single offset vs repeating array, how the offset guide renders, and whether it replaces or coexists with the direction-track parallel. Reuse the direction-acquire plumbing (`align_controller.py` flavor="direction", `align_engine.py` parallel ray) but the ray becomes an offset line (parallel at distance D) and the HUD field is a perpendicular offset. `align_engine.py`, `align_controller.py`, `model_space.py`, `dynamic_input.py`, `settings/panes.py`, `docs/specs/align-placement.md`.
- [ ] [type:feature] ALIGN: discoverability + persistent render for direction-acquire (Parallel) [P2] [subject:UX]
  - Details: user, 2026-08-26 ALIGN smoke test. A direction-acquire (dwell an object's body → acquire its direction for a Parallel guide) currently shows no `+` marker (markers are only drawn for point-acquires, which have a point; a direction-acquire has `point=None`) — so acquiring a direction is invisible. Worse, tracking guides only render when the cursor is snapped to them, so a fixed parallel guide (anchored at the from-point) is invisible until you happen to move onto it. In scope: (1) a distinct direction-acquired indicator (e.g. a "∥" glyph on the acquired reference segment) so the acquire is visible; (2) persistently render the fixed parallel guide (dashed construction line through the from-point) once a direction is acquired + a from-point exists, instead of only-when-snapped; consider whether other acquired guides (extension/perpendicular) should also show a faint persistent path (weigh clutter — likely keep those snap-only). `align_controller.py`, `model_view.py`, `model_space.py`, `docs/specs/align-placement.md`.
- [ ] [type:feature] Re-evaluate the April pipe-3D work (branch `feature/pipe-3d-fixes`, 10 unmerged commits — kept as reference) [P3] [subject:Sprinkler Design]
  - Details: verified 2026-08-25 that NONE of it is on `main` (not superseded): 3D-vector geometry checks (backtrack/collinear/4th-branch/wye converted to 3D vectors; new `cad_math` utils `unit_vector_3d`/`dot_3d`/`angle_between_3d`/`outward_vectors_3d`), vertical riser fittings (`tee_vertical`/`cross_vertical` + SVGs for through-risers — likely valuable for the AHJ riser deliverable), Z-stacked riser-node movement (`z_hint` disambiguation in `find_nearby_node`), contextual `snap_point_45` (uses the reference pipe, not `pipes[0]`), and routing all pipe creation through `add_pipe()`. The branch is 1001 commits behind → do NOT merge/cherry-pick; re-implement the wanted pieces fresh against current `main`, using the branch as reference. First step: decide which of these features are still wanted. `cad_math.py`, `fitting.py`, `node.py`, `pipe.py`, `model_space.py`.

Under the same toggle XOR Shift rule (D139), inside the placement's undo push via `ConstraintController.auto_join`. **Join priority (user 2026-10-27):** where a model element (wall, later floor) and reference geometry (gridline) offer the same point, the auto join takes the element, e.g. a new wall at the shared end of a wall drawn along a gridline joins the wall. - [ ] [type:maint] Wall polish cleanup: one `intersect_lines`, named eps constants, drop the `_rebuild_path` alias [P3] [subject:Code Quality]
  - Details: wall polish T1-4 quality review (2026-10-23). (1) Line intersection is implemented three times: `wall_outline.intersect_lines`, `wall.py _intersect_lines`, `geometry_intersect.line_line_intersection_unbounded` -- keep one. (2) Magic eps `1e-18` / `1e-10` / `1e-9` in `wall.py` / `wall_outline.py` -> named constants in `constants.py`; mid-block `sip` import -> module level. (3) `_rebuild_path` survives as an alias of `invalidate_geometry`; callers `manip_handle.py`, `sketch_adapters.py` (2 sites), `tool_geometry.py`, `scene_tools.py` -> migrate and delete it. (4) `level_manager` still imports `Z_CAT_*` names unused since S1-F1 moved the table to `constants.Z_CATEGORY_BY_CLASS`. (5) The three underlay paint overrides (pixmap / group / placeholder, T6-F2) plus `WaterSupply` repeat one "clear State_Selected on paper" body -> one mixin. (6) `_geometry_key` uses `id(scene)` (reuse after GC is theoretical). Parity: the wall polish guard files pass unchanged.
- [ ] [type:maint] Wall polish guard gaps [P3] [subject:Testing]
  - Details: wall polish reviews (2026-10-23/24). (1) G1 L-butt / Right waiver asserts no pixel result -- add the pixel check. (2) No paper-viewport isolation guard for the outline (a sheet viewport with isolation on must stroke only the isolated walls' boundary) (T1-4 spec#5 = VC9 M7). (3) No mutual-tee guard where one wall's end is partnered elsewhere (S1 review). (4) `_clip_tee_extension` falls back silently to the unclipped body when the clip is not one polygon -- log it, and pin the fallback with a test. `tests/test_wall_polish_*.py`, `tests/test_wall_polish_smoke_fixes.py`.
- [ ] [type:bug] Hidden walls rebuild their outline against the whole wall pool on every level switch (O(N^2)) [P3] [subject:Performance]
  - Details: wall polish T1-4 quality#3 (2026-10-23). A wall made invisible by `LevelManager.apply_to_scene` still scans every wall for partners when invalidated; skip the pool scan while not visible (its outline is unused) and rebuild on show. Measure first on a many-level project (Phase 5 Performance rule). `wall.py` (`invalidate_geometry`, partner search).
- [ ] [type:bug] Every wall rebuilds its outline O(N^2) after load / paste, from Qt's polish visibility notice [P2] [subject:Performance]
  - Details: HF5 VC9 debugger (2026-10-24), pre-existing since 312c4a79 (wall polish D2/D3/D9). After `addItem`, Qt's queued `QGraphicsScenePrivate::_q_polishItems` sends `ItemVisibleChange` / `ItemVisibleHasChanged` to every not-explicitly-hidden item; `wall.py` `itemChange` (~:510-513) treats the synthetic `ItemVisibleHasChanged` as a real visibility flip and calls `invalidate_geometry()` -> `_rebuild_outline` per wall, each scanning the whole pool (300 walls: 358,800 `nearest_centerline_point` calls, ~4.3 s on the first event-loop pass after a project opens). Fix: invalidate only when visibility actually changed (record `isVisible()` on `ItemVisibleChange`, compare on `HasChanged`); re-check level switch / viewport isolation still rejoin (wall polish D3/D4). Bench at base first (Phase 5 Performance rule); probe `C:/Users/gvulcano/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/D--Custom-Code-FirePro3D/a9d99a98-0ee6-4091-b0d0-db503ec89095/scratchpad/test_dbg_rep0.py`. Related: the hidden-walls level-switch line above. `wall.py`.
- [ ] [type:bug] Section views miter walls using the plan's visibility pool [P3] [subject:CAD]
  - Details: wall polish VC9 M1 (2026-10-23). `section_scene.py` (~1324, ~1542) reads `mitered_quad`, whose join pool is now visible walls only (D-AC2), so a wall on a hidden level draws unmitered in a section. Give the section its own pool (walls in the section's range), or an outline query that takes the pool. Repro first.
- [ ] [type:bug] Wall tee edge cases: mitered host end, mutual-tee host choice, Pass-2 snap past the far face [P3] [subject:CAD]
  - Details: wall polish S1 / F9 reviews (2026-10-24). (1) `_clip_tee_extension` clips to the host's raw `quad_points()`, so a tee landing inside the host's mitered end wedge has its extension cut and may stroke an edge inside the host body. (2) `_mutual_tee` checks geometry only, not whether the other wall would pick this wall as its host; a nearer host elsewhere can leave both ends capped. (3) Placement Pass 2 can snap a free end up to 40 mm beyond the host's far face onto the click line (pre-existing). (4) A wall drawn a few mm short of a host's free end tees and leaves an empty outer corner square; user ruling 2026-10-24: acceptable, since corner snapping now catches it -- revisit if it shows up in real drawings. `wall.py`, `wall_placement_controller.py`.
- [ ] [type:feature] Pasted / duplicated / arrayed walls join and take category defaults like drawn walls [P3] [subject:CAD]
  - Details: SB2b T6 review M4 (2026-10-24). The L1410 `paste_items` wall branch (`model_space.py`) mirrors `scene_io.load_from_file`: no tee/miter join pass and no `apply_category_defaults`, unlike the wall tool (`wall_placement_controller.py`). A Duplicate whose endpoint lands on another wall does not tee or miter until the walls are touched. Decide whether paste should run the tool's join step (and on which walls: copies only, or copies + the walls they now touch). `model_space.py` `paste_items`, `wall.py` join helpers.
  - +1 occurrence 2026-10-26 (Editor/Build slice 1 smoke, user-visible): a Duplicate of a wall paints red (its stored colour) with the built-in diagonal By-element hatch instead of the Display Manager section Fill Type, until any DM change re-applies the category. Probe: placed wall `_display_section_type`/`_display_color`/`_display_fill_color` set, pasted copy all None. User ruling: fold this fix into the slice 2 run (see that line). Probe rooms / floor slabs / roofs / openings in `paste_items` too.
- [ ] [type:bug] Duplicate Level leaves copied pipe-network nodes on the source level [P2] [subject:CAD]
  - Details: SB2b T6 fix round (2026-10-24), pre-existing (the node branch of `paste_items` is unchanged since `bfad6b6e`). `copy_items_to_level` swaps `active_level` to the target, but the node branch sets `node.level = obj["level"]` from the record and keeps `ceiling_level`, so copied nodes (and the pipes/sprinklers on them) stay on the source level and `z_pos` follows the source ceiling. Apply the T6 user ruling "retarget source refs" (refs that name the source level move to the new level; other refs kept) via `_retarget_level_refs`. Knock-on: at the same elevation (Duplicate Level's default) `find_nearby_node` reuses the original nodes, so no sprinkler is created and a design area on the level is skipped; once fixed, add a Duplicate Level design-area guard to `tests/test_sb2b_paste_l1410.py`. Repro first. `model_space.py` `paste_items` node branch, `copy_items_to_level`.
- [ ] [type:bug] Wall `boundingRect` has no pen padding [P3] [subject:CAD]
  - Details: wall polish VC9 M3 (2026-10-23, pre-existing). `boundingRect` comes from the outline body without half the stroke width, so the outer half of the outline stroke can leave repaint trails when a wall moves. Pad by the max pen width. `wall.py`.
- [ ] [type:bug] Flaky: `test_selection_readouts_live_drag.py` body / arc mid-drag readouts fail in a combined batch [P3] [subject:Testing]
  - Details: wall polish keep-green (2026-10-23): `test_body_drag_readout_follows_mid_drag` and `test_arc_body_drag_moves_angular_readout_mid_drag` failed once in a 24-file batch on a 1.4 GB-free host, then passed on the batch re-run, alone, and in the batch prefix at base `c9a5bddd` and HEAD. Likely real-cursor / timing interference; cf. the `_NoRealMouse` filter in `tests/test_constraint_live_drag.py`.

Depends on the floor-opening build landing. `scene_tools.py`, `floor_opening.py`, `floor_slab.py`. ref: docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-30-floor-opening-design.md A2.

Set the elevation zone class on that path (or rule that CUT is intended) and guard it through `section_scene_for`. Depends on the floor-opening build landing. `section_scene.py`; ref docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-30-floor-opening-design.md A4.

## Model Space Containment Contract (2026-09-16 design grill — follow-ups)

> Design landed: `docs/specs/model-space-containment-contract.md` (proposal) + forged `docs/specs/feature-system.md`. The contract makes Model Space **placement-only** (Features + Block instances + Underlays; no loose geometry/markup/text), moves all markup/text to Paper Space, reframes **Feature = composes Blocks** (supersedes "siblings"), makes 2D primitives definition-local/level-less, adds **Text as the 9th primitive** (unified data model), dissolves the Create tab, and clean-drops legacy loose geometry on load. Ribbon mockup: `docs/mockups/ribbon-containment-contract.html`. **Implementation is deferred; these are the next steps.** Superseded/relocated existing items are enumerated in the contract's "Task hit-list".

- [ ] [type:feature] Reference-graphic unification — deferred follow-ups (post core-internal slice) [P2] [subject:Architecture]
  - Details: the core-internal re-home LANDED 2026-09-17 (`feat/reference-graphic-unification`; core-internal slice in todo_closed.md — Underlay re-homed on a reference `BlockDefinition`, batched render/snap repointed at it, transparent migration). Remaining pieces, each its own slice: **(4b) curve-fidelity import flip** — flip underlay import to `_preserve_curves=True` after verifying snap parity on parametric (arc/spline/ellipse) underlay geoms in `UnderlaySnapIndex._geom_bounds` + `snap_engine._collect_from_geom` (closes RD1; `dwg_converter.append_geom_to_path` already renders them); **(R5) selection de-prioritization + "lock in place" toggle** — depends on selection-mode ranking; **edit-underlay-in-Block-Editor** — rep-2 materialization, its own perf problem; **per-primitive visibility**; **convert/promote underlay↔block**. ref: reference-graphic-model.md (Deferred work + Divergences RD1/RD3/RD4). `underlay_import_dialog.py`, `dxf_import_worker.py`, `snap_engine.py`, `selection-mode`.

- [ ] [type:bug] HF5-F1 — "Duplicate to library" drops a source .fpdb's Fill Type rows; block-drop preview draws a type the project lacks as the grey fallback [subject:CAD] [P3]
  - Details: HF5 Group E review 2026-10-24 (user ruling: follow-up, latent until a shipped/System block carries types). `main.py` ~:5655 and `block_drop.py` ~:40 call `block_library.load_block_file_with_bundle`, which discards the file's pending Fill Types; `save_to_library` -> `fill_types.bundle_fill_type_rows` writes rows only for ids in the project table, so the copy keeps `fill_type` ids with no rows ("? id" on the next load). Fix: main.py calls `load_block_file_with_types` and passes the pending types through `block_editor.run_duplicate_dialog` / `save_to_library` (an `extra_types=` param) into `bundle_fill_type_rows`; the preview may overlay them for resolution. Never add pending types to the project table from read-only callers. Guard: Duplicate of a typed System block -> the copy's `.fpdb` carries the type row; RED without the pass-through.

Supersedes the former "Import fills — detect + preserve…" feature (PDF half). Perf bar: underlay with fills ≤ 1.5× stroke-only frame time on the real FS plan PDF. Guards G10, G12. Fix `underlay-workflow.md` §16.3. Depends HF3, HF5. `pdf_import_worker.py`, `geometry_import.py`, `dwg_converter.py`, `underlay_controller.py`, `underlay_cache.py`, `block_import_dialog.py`. Drafting patterns follow the end-type rule (linetypes.md ET1-1 / ET1-3): authored in printed mm; model canvases (plan, detail, Block Editor) show them at printed mm x the project drawing scale (`ScaleManager.drawing_scale`); sheet viewports and PDF plot true printed mm in every viewport.

- [ ] [type:feature] System Settings toggle for the zoomed-out hatch tone (D-A35 LOD) [P2] [subject:CAD]
  - Details: UP1 smoke, 2026-10-19. Zoomed out, a fine pattern switches to a flat tone (`HATCH_LOD_TONE` 35 % of the pattern colour, drawn by `hatch_render._tone` from `stamp_lattice`). Over a background layer this reads as a solid fill: blue on grey shows as flat purple. The user asked for a System Settings toggle. Ruling (AskUserQuestion, 2026-10-19): **on = today's behaviour** (the default). **Off = lines until too dense**: drop the `HATCH_LOD_MAX_CELLS` (20k) cap, but **keep** the `HATCH_LOD_MIN_CELL_PX` (2 device px) floor, so sub-pixel cells still draw the tone. The D-A35 perf bar binds the default only; measure and state the cost when off (Phase 5 Performance rules). It should apply to loose shapes, block instances and paper alike (one renderer). Live repaint on toggle. Amend `hatch-and-fill.md` D-A35 and the settings spec. `hatch_render.py`, `constants.py`, the System Settings dialog + QSettings key. Tier Medium.

A tile whose only ops are placeholders may also pass. Revisit trigger: picker open feels slow with a large Hatch patterns folder, or a blank pattern shows in a picker. Cache the verdict beside `_PREVIEW_CACHE`; pin the placeholder case with a guard. `hatch_patterns.py`.

The result is a load step plus an edit step. This affects the ribbon (`main.py` `_apply_fill`) and the property panel alike. Reproduce first (library-only pattern, 2 rects, one pick, count `_undo_pos`). Expected: one step.

### Linetypes build (concept: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-02-linetypes-concept-design.md`; what = `docs/specs/linetypes.md` D-L1–D-L23)

> Filed 2026-10-02 by the linetype concept run (grill Q1–Q23 + brainstorm LD-A, LD1–LD7, all ratified). LT1 ∥ LT2; then LT3 → LT4 → LT5 → LT6 → LT7/LT8. Each slice carries its guards (G1–G11 in the concept doc) and its spec amendments ("Cross-spec reconciliation" in `linetypes.md`). End goal (user): build system geometry (gridline, pipe, leader) from primitives.

- [ ] [type:feature] LT8 — Linetype perf bench + fixes: 2,000 linetyped segments + 400 end blocks, pan/zoom ≤ 16 ms/frame and ≤ 1.5× Continuous [subject:CAD] [P1]
  - Details: D-L21; guard G10. Confirm the bench (scene, interaction, threshold) with the user before any fix; A/B on real data. Depends LT6. **From LT2 review (2026-10-04):** per-op canvas width resolution in `BlockInstance.paint` (`stroke_style.canvas_px`: canonical name + table scan + thin-lines check) measured ~5 ms per 2,000 stroke ops (~1/3 of a 16 ms frame); cheap fix if the bench needs it = a per-paint dict cache keyed by `op.weight`. Also: deep-copied undo snapshots cost 164 vs 120 ms/push at 200 defs x 50 prims. **From LT4 (2026-10-06, G3 review M4):** the Block Editor capability frame builds a scratch definition with a fresh uuid on every preview invalidation, so each one adds a `LinetypeDef` LRU entry (bounded, perf-only) -- key the scratch on a stable id or reuse the reading if the bench shows it. **From LTS (2026-10-06):** Fixed (screen-constant) linetypes re-expand on every zoom step; `tests/test_lts_perf.py` shape A (2,000 short raw polyline segments, Block Editor) measured zoom Fixed/Scale ~1.6-2.7x against the 1.5x bar (B and C within theirs) -- the cost is per-segment setup in `linetype_render.expand` (split_at_zero / canonical / length / phase0 per piece), not dash count; the Seg fast path `_walk_seg` already halved it (57.9 -> 27.4 ms per step on that scene). Also: a plan + detail view at different zooms share one per-instance `_lt_exp_cache` slot, so each re-expands a block's Fixed ops. Bench is report-only (host noise 2-4x); confirm metric + A/B before any fix. `linetypes.md` LTS-8.
  - From WM2 (2026-10-07, `tests/test_wm2_perf.py`): a placement Linetype override on 2,000 small instances painted ~1.75× the same scene As Authored (20 large: ~1.3×) — the linetype renderer's own cost; on a loaded machine (±30 %). Input for the D-L21 bar.
  - From LT5 (2026-10-08, `tests/test_lt5_perf.py`, report-only): ends / end-less after the cheap fixes (`f64e3df2`, median of 3) = raw 1.65×, block 2.34×, raw line-weight 1.69× vs a 1.3× target; end-less scenes ≤ 1.1× base. 1.3× is unreachable for blocks with per-end vector fills (a glyph-free stub is still 1.37×) → batch a placement's canvas end fills into one cached path per (ops, k); also keep resolved ends in `BlockInstance._end_pad_rows` for paint reuse; skip `badge_pad_px` / the second `scene_hit_width` in the raw `_ends_rect` unless an end is missing.

- [ ] [type:bug] Loose line overshoots its endpoints by half the line weight (square pen cap) [P2] [subject:CAD]
  - Details: SB1c smoke, 2026-10-23. A single Line-tool line drawn from the origin paints past its endpoint by half the weight. Wanted: a free end stops flat at the endpoint (FlatCap); where two strokes share a coincident endpoint the corner closes (join or cap per joint, not overshoot). Check the block-instance stroke path (`block_instance.py` paint, `QPen(op.pen)`) and the paper / PDF output for the same cap. Note ET1-3: "a weight-tracking cap is a pen property" (`linetypes.md`). `geometry_2d.py`, `crisp_stroke.py`, `block_instance.py`.

## Ribbon overhaul

- [ ] [type:maint] `can_start("array")` serialises the whole selection on every plan selection change [P3] [subject:Performance]
  - Details: Editor/Build slice 1 VC9 seam review (2026-10-26). `MainWindow._refresh_modify_buttons` -> `ModifyToolsController.can_start` -> `_refusal_hint` -> `_array_copyable` builds `_clipboard_item_dicts` for every selected item; ~5.7 ms for all 11 tools at 500 walls, but Ctrl+A on a large project pays a full serialisation per selection change. Fix: an any()-style early-exit predicate for `can_start` (first copyable item, design-area member rule kept), full list stays in `start()`. Bench first (Phase 5 Performance rule).

## Gridline Revit-aligned UX re-architecture

- [ ] [type:feature] Per-view datum extents slice 1 -- per-view gridline record + plan / detail 2D/3D ends + the 2D/3D grip tag [P2] [subject:CAD]
  - Details: per-view datum design grill 2026-10-30 (Q3-Q5, Q11-Q13, ratified). Override unit = the view instance (`PlanView`, `DetailMarker`; never the sheet viewport -- every viewport of a view renders it identically, view-relationships §7.4). Per END, per view: extent (end position), bubble on/off, bubble standoff; model-only: line origin / angle, label, lock, graphic override. Ends start 3D (= today's model fields, a drag moves every view); a Revit-style clickable "3D"/"2D" tag beside each end grip (manipulator-drawn, house selection style, only while selected, never plots) detaches that end in this view. A 2D end is stored as an offset ALONG the line (keyed by gridline `uid`), so a model move / rotate carries it; a model length change moves only 3D ends; a 2D end may exceed the model extent; gridline delete drops every view's record in the same undo step. Properties panel: read-only "Extents: Model / This view (start, end)" row. Multi-select + drag one end moves the matching end (same end, same view, same 2D/3D state) of every selected grid. Detail views start from MODEL extents, not the parent plan's 2D layout (Q8). Guards (Q15): a 2D drag in the Level 2 tab leaves the Level 1 painted end / bubble unchanged and moves Level 2 (also through save -> reopen and undo); two sheet viewports (L1, L2) plot different bubble positions in an exported PDF; moving the grid keeps a detail's 2D offset; delete + undo restores the records; user smoke on a multi-level file. `_rebuild_geometry()` stays the sole writer -- it must resolve the effective (view) ends. Depends SB4b (plan bubble -> System block). Spec: grid-system.md + view-relationships §7.4 (row 6 annotations becomes real). `gridline.py`, `level_manager.py` (`PlanView`), `detail_view.py`, `selection_manipulator.py`, `paper_space.py`, `scene_io.py`.
- [ ] [type:feature] Per-view datum extents slice 2 -- sections / elevations: gridline model vertical extent, level-datum 2D/3D ends, uid-keyed persisted records [P2] [subject:CAD]
  - Details: per-view datum design grill 2026-10-30 (Q6, Q7, ratified). Same 2D/3D model in vertical views. Gridline end: 3D = a new MODEL vertical extent (top / bottom, "auto" = full building height until first set) shared by every section / elevation showing that grid; 2D = this view only -- the existing `SectionMarker.gridline_z_overrides` becomes the 2D record, re-keyed from label to gridline `uid` (labels can duplicate; migrate label keys on load). Level datum ends (`ElevDatumItem`): per-end 2D/3D; 3D = a model horizontal extent on the level shared by every view showing it ("auto" = span of visible grids, today's behaviour); 2D record persisted on the `SectionMarker` (today session-only). Guards: a 3D-end drag changes every section showing the grid; a 2D-end drag changes only this section; old label-keyed files load. Depends slice 1, SB4c (elevation bubble + datum head -> System blocks). Spec: grid-system.md §8.3 / §8.4 / §8.5, section-view-subsystem.md §8. `section_scene.py`, `section_marker.py`, `gridline.py`, `level_manager.py`.
- [ ] [type:feature] Per-view datum extents slice 3 -- display-only auto-trim of grids / level datums at the crop (detail crop, section crop box, sheet viewport crop_rect) [P2] [subject:CAD]
  - Details: per-view datum design grill 2026-10-30 (Q8, Q9, ratified). A gridline (or level datum in a section) whose end falls outside the shown crop draws to the crop edge with its bubble / head at that edge instead of being clipped away; display-only -- no per-view record written until the user drags an end. Applies to detail crop rects, section crop boxes and a sheet viewport's `crop_rect` (two viewports of one view with different crops may therefore place bubbles differently -- derived, not stored). Independent of slices 1/2/4; may ship first. Guards: a grid crossing a detail crop paints / plots its bubble at the crop edge and writes no record; a viewport crop does the same in the exported PDF. `gridline.py`, `paper_display.py`, `paper_space.py`, `detail_view.py`, `section_scene.py`. ref: paper-space §6.2 / §9.9.1.
- [ ] [type:feature] Per-view datum extents slice 4 -- Propagate Extents + Reset to model extents [P2] [subject:CAD]
  - Details: per-view datum design grill 2026-10-30 (Q10, ratified). Propagate Extents: copy the selected grids' 2D ends from this view to views picked in a checklist dialog (plan / detail -> other plan / detail views; section -> parallel sections), one undo step. Reset to model extents: right-click a grid end or the whole grid -> drop this view's 2D record (back to 3D), one undo step. Guards: propagate writes the targets' records and paints them; each op is one undo step. Depends slice 1 (slice 2 for sections).
- [ ] [type:design] Revit-style alignment lock for grid ends (aligned bubbles move together until unlocked), built on the parametric constraint system [P3] [subject:CAD]
  - Details: per-view datum design grill 2026-10-30 (Q13): v1 ships multi-select end drag only; the auto-grouped alignment lock is deferred here so it comes from the constraint system (parametric-constraint-system.md) instead of a one-off lock. Revisit after slice 1.
- [ ] [type:feature] Per-view visibility of plan view markers (elevation / section / detail markers hidden in a chosen view) [P3] [subject:Architecture]
  - Details: per-view datum design grill 2026-10-30 (Q2): plan view markers were kept out of the per-view extents work. Generalize the per-viewport `SheetViewData.hidden_detail_ids` (paper_space.py `_toggle_hidden_detail`) into a view-owned hidden-marker set (override unit = the view instance, view-relationships §7.4) covering `SectionMarker`s too. Related: "print on sheets" toggle (L9), view-owned per-view underlay visibility.
- [ ] [type:feature] App-wide Y-up display sweep for property panels [P3] [subject:UX]
  - Details: property panels currently show raw Qt Y (down-positive) everywhere (sprinkler/node/etc.); gridline was flipped to up-positive for this branch. Sweep the app to a consistent up-positive display/parse convention. `sprinkler.py`, `node.py`, `property_manager.py`, ….
- [ ] [type:feature] Perpendicular bubble elbow/leader for gridlines [P3] [subject:CAD]
  - Details: jog a bubble off the line with a leader (Revit "add elbow"); per-view leader independence. `gridline.py`, `paper_space.py`.
  - +1 occurrence (per-view datum design grill 2026-10-30, Q4): the elbow joins the per-END, per-VIEW set (with extent, bubble on/off, standoff) of the "Per-view datum extents slice 1" record -- build it on that record, not as a model-only field.
- [ ] [type:feature] On-canvas rotate handle + Display-Manager linetype property for gridlines [P3] [subject:CAD]
  - Details: angle currently edited in the panel; linetype is fixed dash-dot. `gridline.py`, `model_space.py`, `display_manager.py`.
- [ ] [type:feature] Extend the inference engine to wall/pipe/sprinkler providers and consumers [P3] [subject:Architecture]
  - Details: the engine is generic but only gridlines provide references + only gridline placement/grip consume it; add wall/pipe/sprinkler providers + placement-tool + body-drag consumers, and the deferred guide types (wall-proximity, extension, equal-spacing) per `inferred-dimension-driven-placement.md`. `inference_engine.py`, `wall.py`, `pipe.py`, `model_space.py`.
- [ ] [type:maint] Extract + generalize the Dynamic Input engine into a reusable non-modal HUD (§4) [P2] [subject:Architecture]
  - Details: user, 2026-08-16 — `_DynInput` is a modal `QDialog` nested inside a `model_space.py` method, redefined/instantiated ad hoc per mode; extract to a reusable top-level controller/widget (`dynamic_input.py`), generalize to all placement modes (pipe/wall/arc/rectangle), and (design decision) move from Tab-triggered modal to a live non-modal HUD per §4.3/§4.5; coordinate with the inference engine at the Model_Space seam (typed value overrides snap/inference). Update `inferred-dimension-driven-placement.md §4` in place. `model_space.py`, new `dynamic_input.py`, `model_view.py`. ref: inferred-dimension-driven-placement §4. Lineage: `feat/dynamic-input-hud` D3 scope COMPLETE 2026-08-20 (plan/findings ledger gitignored); shipped one-HUD lifecycle (S1–S3), Line/Rectangle/Circle schemas, polyline, T16 (gridline offset/array), T15 (move + a `GridlineItem.translate` fix), T11 (cycling off Tab, since replaced by Space), T17/T21/T22 (spec §4 rewrite + SPEC-INDEX). HUD clients T18 (wall) 2026-08-24 + T19 (pipe) 2026-09-03 landed (see `todo_closed.md`). Known gap: offset_side/rotate/scale/fillet/chamfer lost their Tab exact-input — needs re-homing.

- [ ] [type:bug] Retire the orphan `MainWindow.view` that breaks `views()[0]` consumers [P2] [subject:Architecture]
  - Details: `main.py:400` — a `Model_View` attached to the plan scene but never parented, never shown, never added to `central_tabs`, so it is `scene.views()[0]`. It broke the HUD in smoke test (built correctly inside an invisible widget tree). Worked around locally by selecting the first visible view, but ~12 other `views()[0]` uses in `model_space.py` have the same latent bug — dialog parents at ~2190/6438/8106 parent onto the orphan, and zoom-scale reads at ~3634/6783/7227/7749 read its transform. Retiring it (or never attaching it to the scene) fixes all of them at once. `main.py`, `model_space.py`.
  - 2026-10-10 (placement batch review): `MainWindow._active_view()` returns this hidden view for the plan, so focus hand-offs and anything else using it act on an invisible widget; the placement batch worked around it in `_on_block_activated` (current tab's own view). Retiring the orphan should repoint `_active_view` too.

Two independent pen-construction sites that must stay visually consistent; consider a shared pen/path-builder helper so they can't drift (the original thick-line bug existed in only one of them).
- [ ] [type:bug] Include underlays in the undo system [P2] [subject:CAD]
  - Details: `_capture_network` / `_restore_network` intentionally exclude underlays; importing an underlay is not undoable (underlay persists through undo/redo). Quick fix (push_undo_state after import) done 2026-04-24; full fix requires serializing underlays into undo snapshots and clearing/restoring them in `_restore_network`. `model_space.py:_capture_network`, `model_space.py:_restore_network`.

## Snapping Engine Roadmap (from `docs/specs/snapping-engine.md` §12)

- [ ] [type:feature] Underlay snap glyphs orient to the DXF/PDF segment, not the underlay group [P3] [subject:CAD]
  - Details: follow-up of the 2026-09-29 glyph-orientation task (snapping-engine §9.2 "Known gap"). `_query_underlay_snaps` calls `ctx.check(..., group, ...)` with no segment, so `snap_tangent_deg` falls back to the group's scene rotation. Carry the index geometry's segment (as `source_lines`) on underlay candidates — check the trace path doesn't then light up differently. `snap_engine.py`, `underlay_snap_index.py`.
- [ ] [type:maint] F3 integration test on real keypress [P3] [subject:Testing]
  - Details: QTest.keyClick did not dispatch through QAction shortcut on headless Windows; investigate pytest-qt / qtbot or alternate dispatch.
- [ ] [type:design] Spec session: pipe-with-fitting named targets [P2] [subject:CAD]
  - Details: ref: snap-spec §8.3.
- [ ] [type:bug] Phase-4 intersections on curves use the Bézier control polygon [P3] [subject:CAD]
  - Details: found 2026-09-24 (snap-polish 1b, S4). `snap_engine.py` phase-4 (`_check_geometry_intersections` path-element extraction) intersects raw path elements incl. Bézier control points, so a line crossing an arc/ellipse/spline gets no intersection snap at the true crossing. Same flattening fix as S4 (`toSubpathPolygons`). Also: DXF curve control points emitted as endpoint snaps. ref: snapping-engine §6.1.
- [ ] [type:bug] Block-compiled text drops its border frame + fill [P3] [subject:CAD]
  - Details: found 2026-09-24 (snap-polish 1b, S6) — unconfirmed: a TextItem's frame border/fill never reach the compiled block render_ops (only glyph outlines). Reproduce first. `block_definition.py`, `text_item.py`. ref: block-system.
  - +1 occurrence 2026-10-24 (SB2a smoke, user): reproduced. A bordered text in the Block Editor shows no frame on the placed instance in plan. Probe (`border: True` text prim, plain and `@[W]`): `inst.render_ops()` kinds = `['text']` only, identical at base `6e41d69b` and HEAD `595cdd8f` (VC7). Static texts compile via `BlockDefinition._compile` (glyph TEXT op); field texts go through `bound_prims` → `BlockInstance` bound ops, so both paths need the border. Same work as the WM-12 feature line "Text-box border renders inside placed blocks" (fold the two together when picked).
- [ ] [type:bug] Placed blocks (tags) draw under walls and openings in plan [P2] [subject:CAD]
  - Details: SB2a smoke, user, 2026-10-24: a placed tag block's text sits below the walls and doors. User expectation: annotations and tags draw above all model geometry. `view-relationships.md §7.3` says a block instance draws "above building geometry at equal elevation", but `LevelManager.apply_to_scene` `_apply_elev_z` takes z from `max(z_range_mm())`. A wall's max is its TOP (3048 mm gives z 30.78), while `BlockInstance.z_range_mm()` is the level plane (gives 0.6), so every block lands under walls (and openings, which are pinned above their wall). Proven pre-existing (VC7): wall z 30.78 / block z 0.6 at base `6e41d69b` and HEAD `595cdd8f`. Decide at 1b: an annotation band (like `_Z_OVERLAY` / `Z_GRIDLINE_BUBBLE`) for tag and annotation blocks vs model-symbol blocks, and how this squares with view-range cutting (a block on a lower level must not float above an upper level's walls). Feeds SB6 placed tags. `level_manager.py`, `block_instance.py`, `constants.py`. ref: view-relationships.md §7.3.
- [ ] [type:feature] Snap trace outlines a TextItem's box when text is the snap source [P3] [subject:UX]
  - Details: found 2026-09-24 (snap-polish G2 review S2). `paint_snap_indicator` trace (snapping-engine §9.2.1) has no TextItem branch, so snapping to a text corner/mid/centre shows the marker but no box trace. Add a branch drawing the rotated frame polygon. `snap_engine.py`.
- [ ] [type:feature] ALIGN crossings dwell-acquirable [P3] [subject:CAD]
  - Details: found 2026-09-24 (snap-polish 1b, S5) — the dwell machine is fed only the real snap result, so an ALIGN crossing (align_intersection) can't itself be acquired. `align_controller.py`, `model_space.py`. ref: align-placement.
- [ ] [type:design] Roof placement has no governing spec section [P3] [subject:Docs]
  - Details: found 2026-09-24 (snap-polish Task 13). Roof polygon/rect placement (click flow, close → RoofDialog, Ctrl, HUD absence — `_move_roof` publishes no placement state) is described nowhere; forge a section in `wall-room-floor-system.md` (orphan-gate on next roof touch).
- [ ] [type:bug] Spline endpoint snaps include off-curve interior control points [P3] [subject:CAD]
  - Details: found 2026-09-24 (snap-polish Task 13). `snap_engine._collect` SplineItem branch emits every `grip_points()` control point as `endpoint`; interior control points of a Bézier/NURBS lie off the curve → snaps to empty space. Emit only the true curve ends (+ maybe on-curve knots). `snap_engine.py`. ref: snapping-engine §5 note 11.
- [ ] [type:feature] ALIGN suspended while SNAP is off — hide acquired markers + dim the ALIGN pill [P3] [subject:CAD]
  - Details: from the 2026-09-26 design grill (ratified, `align-placement.md` §6.1). Engine gate already suspends ALIGN when F3 is off; build the visible half: (1) gate the `+` acquired markers in `Model_View.drawForeground` on SNAP as well as ALIGN — do NOT clear `AlignController.acquired` in `toggle_snap` (hide, restore on F3 on); (2) ALIGN footer pill keeps its checked (F11) state but renders dimmed with tooltip "ALIGN suspended — SNAP (F3) is off" while SNAP is off — wire `snapToggled` into `_update_guides_indicator` / `footer.set_align_on`. P4 first step: probe that a dynamic-property selector (e.g. `[suspended="true"]`) combined with `:checked` actually renders on the footer `QToolButton` (repolish on change; verify by pixel sampling — unstyled pseudo-states render as base). Guards (VC3): acquire a point, F3 off → marker not painted + pill dimmed/tooltip; F3 on → same marker back. Needs a user smoke (visual).
- [ ] [type:maint] Flaky `test_elev_selection_interaction::test_empty_click_deselects` under real mouse input [P3] [subject:Testing]
  - Details: found 2026-09-25 (snap-polish final suite, 1/5337; same tree passed the previous run, 3/3 isolated, passes in the full 83-file prefix; no elevation/halo/conftest code touched by the batch). Mechanism proven: the elevation press is HALO-committed (commits the current `halo_item()`), so a hover over the gridline arriving between the test's `_move((5,5))` and its press — e.g. the real OS cursor over the suite's visible window — keeps the gridline selected. Harden: have the test (or `ElevationView` press) re-pick HALO at the press position when it differs from the last hover point; consider whether the live press should also re-pick (a real press always follows a hover at the same point, so likely test-only). `tests/test_elev_selection_interaction.py`, `elevation_view.py`, `halo_selection.py`.
- [ ] [type:bug] Cursor snap runs on every middle-button pan step (pan lag) [P3] [subject:Performance]
  - Details: found 2026-09-24 (snap-polish G4 RR bench, pre-existing). During a pan, each replayed mouse move runs `get_effective_position` (full `find()`, O(n) on the NoIndex scene) + repaint: ~170 ms/step at 2k items headless. Skip snapping while a pan is in progress (middle button held / view panning flag). Bench first on a real file. `model_view.py`, `model_space.py`.
- [ ] [type:bug] Underlay intersection snapping bails in very dense regions [P3] [subject:CAD]
  - Details: phase-4 returns early once segment extraction exceeds `_PHASE4_MAX_SEGMENTS` (256) to bound O(n²) pairing, so a cursor over a dense DXF area gets no intersection snap at all. Pre-existing for every entity type. Decide whether section Hide persists (skip sources whose override is False in each projection) or is view-transient; then fix + guard: hide a wall proxy, trigger `rebuild()`, assert the proxy is absent / hidden. `firepro3d/section_scene.py`. ref: section-view-subsystem spec (via SPEC-INDEX).

## Wall, Room & Floor Slab Follow-Ups (from `docs/specs/wall-room-floor-system.md` §13–§14)

- [ ] [type:maint] Pre-existing spec drift found at the floor-opening Account [P3] [subject:Docs]
  - Details: VC10 list, 2026-10-31 (Account at `1c1e59c6`). (1) wall-room-floor-system §3.3 Z table and §11.6 still quote the retired fixed z values (-80 / -60 / -50 / -45); runtime z is elevation-based (owned by view-relationships §7.3) -- link, don't restate. (2) §11.10 names `elevation_scene._project_floor_slabs`; the module is now `section_scene.py`. (3) §7.2 / §7.13 say "Feature > Category > Type"; feature-system F4 settled "Feature > Family > Type". (4) The Model Browser never lists a wall's blank openings (noted in model-browser §3.3) -- decide whether it should. (5) snapping-engine §5 has no `FloorSlab` row though slabs take the generic path. (6) `model-space-architecture.md` (feature placement controller) not re-audited for the Floor host strategy. `docs/specs/`.

- [ ] [type:bug] Level dropdown in Properties hides a missing level and can't re-select the level it falls back to [P2] [subject:UX]
  - Details: floor-opening smoke 2026-10-31, `26307 Model_new.FPD`: the Basement Floor Slab stores `top_level: "Level 1"` (deleted), but its Top Level row shows "Basement/Mall". `property_manager.py` level_ref branch: non-editable `QComboBox` + `setCurrentText(missing)` is a no-op, so index 0 shows; picking that entry emits no `currentTextChanged`, so the user must pick another level first. Show the stored value (e.g. "<missing: Level 1>" item) so any real pick applies; guard on a shown panel. Affects every level_ref row. `property_manager.py`.
- [ ] [type:design] Open repair skips floors: a slab Top / Bottom Level naming a missing level stays dangling [P2] [subject:CAD]
  - Details: floor-opening smoke 2026-10-31 (same file). `LevelManager.repair_missing_refs` skips `_visibility_by_zrange` items (wall-room-floor-system.md §11.3 "Floors are skipped"), so the slab's `z_range_mm()` is None: `_apply_z_filter` keeps it visible on every plan, 3D / elevation skip it, and floor-void placement rejects it. A floor has no own level, so the repair target needs a ruling (lowest level, active level, or user prompt). User fixed the file by hand for now. `level_manager.py`, `floor_slab.py`.

- [ ] [type:bug] Display Manager Floor row reverts to not-visible: its checkbox reads the first slab's view-range visibility [P2] [subject:UX]
  - Details: user, 2026-10-22: Floor shows unchecked in the Display Manager and reverts even after checking it and Set as Default. Root-caused read-only (not yet reproduced live): `display_manager._populate_tree` seeds every row from `_read_item_display_state(items[0], key)`, whose `visible` is `item.isVisible()`; floor slabs are `_visibility_by_zrange` and `level_manager._set_level_vis` / `_apply_z_filter` hide a slab outside the view range, so when `scene._floor_slabs[0]` is out of range the row shows unchecked, and OK (`accept`) writes `display/Floor/visible = false` (the user's registry: `visible false`, `default_visible true`). Likely sticky too: applying visible False sets `_display_overrides["visible"]`, which `_set_level_vis` then respects. Same shape for any row whose first item is level/view-range hidden (Wall, Room, ...). Fix: the row's Visible comes from the category setting (QSettings / `_display_overrides`), never from `isVisible()`. Guard: two slabs, the first above the view range; open the DM -> Floor checked; OK -> setting stays true. `firepro3d/display_manager.py`, `firepro3d/level_manager.py`. ref: display-manager spec (via SPEC-INDEX).
  - Repro (user, 2026-10-22): floors are visible when the file opens; open the Display Manager, change nothing, press OK -> the floors disappear. So OK writes the row's mis-read Visible (and applies it); the load path itself is fine.

- [ ] [type:bug] Legacy face-snapped tee endpoints never form room-detection T-nodes [P3] [subject:CAD]
  - Details: `_detect_room_boundary` only tees endpoints within `TOL*3` (6mm) of the host centerline; pre-2026-07-13 files have tee endpoints on the face (~half-thickness away), so their tees never split walls in the room graph (pre-existing, surfaced by the tee-join rework). New walls are fine (centerline snap). Consider a load-time migration (re-snap face-parked tee endpoints to the centerline) or widening the detection band. `model_space.py:_detect_room_boundary`.
  - +1 occurrence 2026-10-24 (wall polish smoke S1, user ruling "revert, file a room bug"): room detection is wrong beyond the band. (1) The tee-split loop skips `src_wall is w`, but `pt_sources` stores the HOST, so every tee node is attached to every wall except its host; that is why partitioned rooms come out as a self-crossing bow-tie (`tests/test_wall_polish_rooms.py` golden `[(0,4000),(3000,0),(3000,4000),(0,0)]` is in that order). (2) With the check flipped to `is not w`, the boundary walk ("tightest CW turn", start from the nearest wall's full edge) returns the OUTER rectangle for a bay click, and a second path returns a half-thickness inset rect (`[(200,200)..(5800,3800)]` for a Left-aligned 200 mm scene). (3) Two 5% end margins (`WallSegment.nearest_face_point` and the split loop's `0.05 < t < 0.95`) disagree with the walls' tee rule since wall polish S1-F2 (`nearest_centerline_point`: whole span except within `MITER_TOL` of an end). An attempted alignment (b0822612, reverted in 63a86f02) turned the big-side click of a near-end partition into no room. Fix all three together; guard = simple polygon (no self-crossing) + exact area on both sides of a mid-span and a near-end (t=0.033) partition, Center/Left/Right; re-baseline the rooms golden.

Sweep all of `firepro3d/` for chrome text and colours that bypass the `theme.py` two-layer tokens: raw hex / named colours (`grey`, `#...`, `rgb(...)`) in `setStyleSheet`, `QColor(...)`/`QPen`/`QBrush`/`QPalette` used for chrome (NOT canvas/geometry entities, which are Display-Manager-owned), and hard-coded `font-size`/`font-family`/point-size literals. Classify chrome (in scope) vs canvas content (out); tokenise the chrome; then extend `tests/test_theme_chrome_hexguard.py` to the full chrome file set so drift stays caught app-wide. Progress 2026-09-06: the UI Design-System task added the house dialog base + kit + 5 migrated dialogs to the hexguard allowlist (7 files) and forged a `test_metrics_drift_guard.py`; the full-app sweep of the remaining chrome modules is still open. `theme.py`, all chrome modules, `tests/test_theme_chrome_hexguard.py`. ref: theming.md.

PARTIAL 2026-09-10: RectangleItem now shows a centre move grip in BOTH states (rotated = parametric grip 8; unrotated box-native = `manip_box_extra_handles()` appended to the rigid set). The general case (multi-select group centre, paper viewport/text, other rigid-fallback selections) is still interior-drag only — generalize via a rigid CENTRE/MOVE handle if wanted app-wide. `selection_manipulator.py`.

- [ ] [type:bug] Ctrl+click over the manipulator frame is swallowed (no additive toggle there) [P2] [subject:UX]
  - Details: PRE-EXISTING, surfaced by the U5 Leg A seam review. The manipulator interior-press guard (`model_space.py` ~4267) routes any press over the frame to the manipulator BEFORE `_press_select_item`, excluding only Shift — so a Ctrl+click additive/toggle landing on the frame never reaches selection once something is selected. Can't simply add Ctrl to the exclusion (a Ctrl+press on a resize handle starts Ctrl-from-centre scale). Needs a design pass distinguishing Ctrl+click-additive (frame interior) from Ctrl+press-on-handle-scale. `model_space.py`, `selection_manipulator.py`.
- [ ] [type:feature] HALO "pick from list" for dense stacks [P3] [subject:UX]
  - Details: deferred from U5 Leg A (part of the user's HALO design). When Spacebar-cycling through many overlapping candidates gets tedious, offer a pick-from-list dropdown at the cursor. `model_space.py`, `model_view.py`. ref: selection-mode §4 (HALO).
- [ ] [type:maint] HALO hover on huge drawings: spatial index for the model scene [P2] [subject:Performance]
  - Details: 2026-09-24 bench (Block Editor, Sample.pdf): the HALO `scene.items(box)` pre-filter is ~50 ms/move @ 20k primitives (~200 ms+ @ 85k) because Model_Space is `NoIndex`; identical on main, px ranking adds only 3–7 ms. NoIndex was chosen deliberately — needs its own perf spike (BspTreeIndex vs a HALO-side cache) on real data. `model_space.py`, `halo_selection.py`.
- [ ] [type:bug] Remaining `views()[0]` zoom reads (render + tool tolerances) [P2] [subject:UX]

In the app `views()[0]` is the vestigial never-shown view frozen at m11 = 1.0 (snapping-engine.md §14.4), so these are world-unit, not screen-constant: `displayable_item.py` + `hatch_patterns.py` hatch tile size, `wall.py` hatch spacing, `pipe.py` endpoint dots, `scene_tools.py` pick tolerances (3 sites). Route through `view_scale.scene_view_scale` (render sites may prefer `painter.deviceTransform()`).

Reproduce with repeated battery runs before changing anything.

Fix the two strings to point at Underlay Manager → Relink… (or add the action where they point). Repro: open a project whose underlay file was moved.

  - Details: new two-boundary slabs don't reliably write `.level`, so ceiling-height can degrade. Not a deserialize-path issue; separate fix. `room.py`. ref: model-space-architecture §4.
  - +1 occurrence 2026-10-24 (SB2b T6 review): confirmed `FloorSlab.level` is never set (always the `init_displayable` default), so `room.py` ~151 and ~311 (`getattr(slab, "level", None) == self._ceiling_level`) subtract the ceiling slab's thickness only when the ceiling level is Level 1. SB2b paste / `_items_on_level` now treat a slab's own level as `_top_level` (LevelManager rename already remaps Top/Bottom) -- compare `slab._top_level` here too, with a room-height guard. Related: an absolute-mode slab (`_top_mode != "level"`) keeps the default `_top_level` "Level 1", so `_items_on_level("Level 1")` / paste `source_level` treat it as on Level 1; guard with `_top_mode == "level"`.

## Import

> PDF Import Polish cluster (2026-08-28, `feat/pdf-import-polish`) SHIPPED + smoke-tested. Governing specs: `underlay-workflow.md` (import) · `snapping-engine.md` · `icon-style-guide.md`. Remaining DWG/multi-layout items below.

- [ ] [type:bug] Block Editor DXF import of a full ellipse mirrors its rotation (30° arrives as 150°) [P3] [subject:CAD]
  - Details: found 2026-10-01 by the scene-tools P1 batch 1b arc-angle census (probe `probe_ell.py` in that session's scratchpad). `dxf_import_worker.py` full-ellipse branch emits `"rotation": -rotation` (Y-flip for the scene), then `geometry_import.py` (~:182) feeds that Y-down angle to the Y-up `EllipseItem`. Fix at one hop only; `test_geometry_import.py:94` pins the ellipse rotation if the fix lands in `geometry_import`. Check periodic DXF splines too (import drops the spline `closed` flag). Convention: `project_rotation_conventions_yup_vs_qt`.
- [ ] [type:feature] DWG import: paper-space viewport compositing [P2] [subject:CAD]
  - Details: Revit DWGs place building geometry and gridline/dimension annotations at separate model-space coordinates, composited via paper-space viewports. Current import shows raw model space where they're physically separated. Need to read viewport clip boundaries + transforms and remap block coordinates to overlay annotations with building geometry. `dxf_preview_dialog.py`, `dwg_converter.py`.
- [ ] [type:maint] Multi-layout import extraction is O(all model space) per layout switch [P2] [subject:CAD]
  - Details: selecting any layout re-walks all ~49,628 model-space entities and explodes every INSERT/HATCH/DIMENSION regardless of viewport (`_entity_in_viewport` returns True for them), producing ~388k explosion geoms filtered down to ~7.5k for a single sheet. Two improvements: (A) extract model space once, filter per layout — cache the unfiltered geoms and apply only the cheap viewport-bounds filter on each layout switch instead of re-extracting; (B) bbox-prefilter blocks — test INSERT/HATCH/DIMENSION extents via `ezdxf.bbox.extents([ent], fast=True)` (generous margin) and skip exploding those outside the viewport. B is the dominant per-layout win and is localized; A helps multi-layout switching. `dxf_import_worker.py:_entity_in_viewport`, `dxf_preview_dialog.py:_DialogExtractWorker._run_inner`, `dwg_converter.py`.

Only `BlockInstance._text_path` applies the DD6 upright turn; `hatch_render._tile_paths` and `end_render` (TEXT branch) draw `op.path` directly. Repro first; route both through one shared helper (block_pose) rather than a third copy.
  - +1 occurrence 2026-10-24 (SB2a smoke border round review, Minor): block text now compiles its box too (`block_definition.text_box_ops`: a FILL op before the glyph TEXT op, a border STROKE after). `hatch_render._tile_paths` collects FILL and TEXT ops into one fill path, so a filled text inside a pattern tile merges its glyphs into the box fill (the text is unreadable). Separate the text glyphs from fills in the same pass that fixes the upright turn. Text in tiles is rare.

Add a hatch-angle guard to `tests/test_block_mirror_scale_explode.py` when fixing.
  - +1 occurrence (HF2 polish, 2026-10-17, VC9 seam review): the hatch ANGLE now differs too — a rotated / mirrored instance turns its hatch (hatch-and-fill D-A11 block-pose exception) while the exploded loose primitives stay scene-aligned, so the lines jump on Explode. Parity would need a per-item pattern angle, which D-A10 rules out — a design fork, not a plain fix.
